### BADGR: Bundle Adjustment Diffusion Conditioned by GRadients for Wide-Baseline Floor Plan Reconstruction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.19340v1)
- **Authors**: Yuguang Li, Ivaylo Boyadzhiev, Zixuan Liu, Linda Shapiro, Alex Colburn
- **Abstract**: Reconstructing precise camera poses and floor plan layouts from wide-baseline RGB panoramas is a difficult and unsolved problem. We introduce BADGR, a novel diffusion model that jointly performs reconstruction and bundle adjustment (BA) to refine poses and layouts from a coarse state, using 1D floor boundary predictions from dozens of images of varying input densities. Unlike a guided diffusion model, BADGR is conditioned on dense per-entity outputs from a single-step Levenberg Marquardt (LM) optimizer and is trained to predict camera and wall positions while minimizing reprojection errors for view-consistency. The objective of layout generation from denoising diffusion process complements BA optimization by providing additional learned layout-structural constraints on top of the co-visible features across images. These constraints help BADGR to make plausible guesses on spatial relations which help constrain pose graph, such as wall adjacency, collinearity, and learn to mitigate errors from dense boundary observations with global contexts. BADGR trains exclusively on 2D floor plans, simplifying data acquisition, enabling robust augmentation, and supporting variety of input densities. Our experiments and analysis validate our method, which significantly outperforms the state-of-the-art pose and floor plan layout reconstruction with different input densities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces BADGR, a novel diffusion-based model for jointly reconstructing camera poses and floor plan layouts from sparsely captured 360° panoramas. Unlike traditional Structure from Motion (SfM) or existing guided diffusion approaches, BADGR is conditioned on dense per-column outputs derived from a single-step Levenberg-Marquardt (LM) optimization. This allows the model to refine poses and layouts from a coarse initial state by predicting camera and wall positions while minimizing reprojection errors. BADGR leverages learned layout-structural constraints during denoising, enabling plausible guesses about spatial relationships even with noisy input features or occluded walls.  The model simplifies data acquisition by training exclusively on 2D floor plans and demonstrated superior performance on pose and floor plan layout reconstruction compared to state-of-the-art methods, particularly with varying input densities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates novelty in several aspects:

    *   A learned bundle adjustment based model built around denoising diffusion probabilistic model trained to reconstruct room layouts and camera poses from sparse RGB panoramas.
    *   A novel way to train a diffusion model where it integrates a non-linear optimizer in its architecture. This also enables generating reasonable guesses for occluded layout sections.
    *   It addresses a challenging problem (wide-baseline floor plan reconstruction) with a learning-based approach.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact of this work is significant because of these points:

    *   The ability to accurately reconstruct floor plans and camera poses from sparse panoramas has numerous applications, including virtual touring, interior design, and autonomous navigation. The reduced requirement for dense capture makes it practical for real-world scenarios.
    *   By formulating the problem as a joint reconstruction and bundle adjustment task, BADGR bridges the gap between traditional geometric optimization and learned priors.
    *   The demonstrated improvement over existing methods on standard datasets suggests a significant advancement in the field.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper provides a well-defined problem statement and a clear explanation of the proposed method.
    *   The experiments are comprehensive, comparing BADGR against multiple baselines and evaluating performance under different input conditions.
    *   The ablation studies demonstrate the importance of different components of the proposed model.
    *   The method demonstrated reasonable guesses for occluded layouts thanks to the generative ability of the denoising diffusion probabilistic model which can be trained with layout-structural constraints.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper relies on an Atlanta World assumption and does not handle curved walls which could limit its applicability in certain environments.
    *   Coarse scene initialization relies on an annotator with a graphics interface to provide global wall connectivity. This can be automated to allow for an end-to-end pipeline.
    *   The method uses manually created floor plans where it could have applied transfer learning from other models trained with a diverse set of floor plans and tested its effectiveness.
    *   While the reprojection is optimized, the results still has inaccuracies, and future efforts could focus on how to mitigate the reprojection errors to improve the results.

*   **Justification for Score:**
Based on the above, I believe a score of 7 is appropriate.

The paper demonstrates genuine novelty in its approach to fusing diffusion models with bundle adjustment for a challenging scene reconstruction task. The performance improvements are significant, and the ablation studies offer valuable insights. However, the limitations related to assumptions on the Atlanta World environments, curved-wall floor plans, and the reliance on non-automation reduce the overall impact. It moves the state-of-the-art forward, but its real-world impact hinges on addressing these limitations in future research.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### QUAD: Quantization and Parameter-Efficient Tuning of LLM with Activation Decomposition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.19353v1)
- **Authors**: Yuxuan Hu, Xiaodong Chen, Cuiping Li, Hong Chen, Jing Zhang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) excel in diverse applications but suffer inefficiency due to massive scale. While quantization reduces computational costs, existing methods degrade accuracy in medium-sized LLMs (e.g., Llama-3-8B) due to activation outliers. To address this, we propose QUAD (Quantization with Activation Decomposition), a framework leveraging Singular Value Decomposition (SVD) to suppress activation outliers for effective 4-bit quantization. QUAD estimates activation singular vectors offline using calibration data to construct an orthogonal transformation matrix P, shifting outliers to additional dimensions in full precision while quantizing rest components to 4-bit. Additionally, QUAD enables parameter-efficient fine-tuning via adaptable full-precision outlier weights, narrowing the accuracy gap between quantized and full-precision models. Experiments demonstrate that QUAD achieves 94% ~ 96% accuracy under W4A4 quantization and 98% accuracy with W4A4/A8 and parameter-efficient fine-tuning for Llama-3 and Qwen-2.5 models. Our code is available at \href{https://github.com/hyx1999/Quad}{repository}.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper, "QUAD: Quantization and Parameter-Efficient Tuning of LLM with Activation Decomposition."

**Summary**

The paper introduces QUAD (Quantization with Activation Decomposition), a novel framework for quantizing Large Language Models (LLMs) while minimizing accuracy degradation. The core idea is to use Singular Value Decomposition (SVD) to decompose activation matrices and identify and isolate "outlier" features into separate, full-precision dimensions. This allows the remaining components of the activations to be quantized aggressively (e.g., 4-bit), reducing computational cost without significantly impacting accuracy. The method also incorporates parameter-efficient fine-tuning by retaining the weights corresponding to the outlier dimensions in full precision, allowing them to act as adaptable parameters. The experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of QUAD on Llama-3 and Qwen-2.5 models, achieving high accuracy even with low-bit quantization. The code is available.

**Critical Evaluation**

**Novelty:**

*   **SVD for Activation Outlier Mitigation:** The application of SVD to activation matrices to isolate and handle outliers is the core novel contribution. While SVD has been used for weight compression, its application to *activations* in the context of quantization appears to be a relatively novel approach.  The method of building a transformation matrix P to shift outliers to extra dimensions is also innovative.
*   **Integration with Existing Techniques:** QUAD is designed to be compatible with and augment existing rotation-based quantization methods (like QuaRot). This "building-upon-existing-work" approach is valuable because it leverages existing research and infrastructure.
*   **Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning with Outlier Weights:** The use of full-precision outlier weights as adaptable parameters for fine-tuning is a clever and practical way to bridge the accuracy gap between quantized and full-precision models.

**Significance:**

*   **Addressing the Activation Outlier Bottleneck:** The paper directly tackles a key challenge in LLM quantization: the difficulty of quantizing activations due to outliers. QUAD offers a potential solution to this problem, enabling more aggressive quantization levels with acceptable accuracy.
*   **Practicality and Efficiency:** The method aims for practicality by being compatible with existing quantization pipelines and offering a parameter-efficient fine-tuning strategy. The claims of improved GEMM efficiency with INT4/INT8 TensorCores are significant for real-world deployment.
*   **Performance on Recent Models:** The paper evaluates QUAD on the latest Llama-3 and Qwen-2.5 models, demonstrating its applicability to current LLM architectures. The reported accuracy numbers are promising and show the method can restore performance lost during quantization.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition and Motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of LLM quantization and the need for better activation handling.
*   **Well-Defined Method:** QUAD is clearly explained, with a good level of detail regarding the SVD decomposition, transformation matrix construction, and parameter-efficient fine-tuning process.  The paper also provides theoretical analyses to support the method.
*   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The experiments cover various LLM families (Llama-3, Qwen-2.5), zero-shot tasks, and generation tasks.  Comparisons with baseline quantization methods are provided.
*   **Code Availability:** The promise of available code is a significant strength, allowing for reproducibility and further research.
*   **Integration with Existing Rotation Methods**: Enhancing existing quantization methods to perform better is always a good strategy.
**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Ablation Studies:** While the paper provides results with and without fine-tuning, more detailed ablation studies would strengthen the analysis. For example: How does accuracy vary with the number of singular vectors removed (the 'r' parameter)? What is the performance impact of the Hadamard transforms vs. using just the SVD-based projection? More analysis of parameter efficiency would also be beneficial.
*   **GEMM Efficiency Analysis:** While the paper claims improved GEMM efficiency with INT4/INT8 TensorCores, there is no micro-level evaluation. Providing actual timing measurements or FLOPs calculations would be valuable. The percentage of operations conducted on INT4/INT8 cores is provided which is helpful, but more direct measures of speedup are necessary.
*   **Efficiency Comparison:** Detailed efficiency analysis comparing to existing quantization methods such as AWQ are not present.
*   **Dependence on Calibration Data:** The method relies on calibration data to estimate activation singular vectors. The sensitivity of QUAD to the size and quality of the calibration data is not thoroughly investigated. How much calibration data is needed to properly estimate SVD?
*   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While the theoretical analysis provided is helpful, it could be more comprehensive. Analyzing how the choice of 'r' affects the theoretical bound of the quantization error would be a very useful addition.
*   **Clarity around Hadamard Transform Usage**: The approach of when to use (or not to use) Hadamard Transform in section 4.1 in the transformer architecture may cause confusion to readers.

**Potential Influence:**

QUAD has the potential to influence the field of LLM quantization by providing a practical and effective method for handling activation outliers. It could lead to:

*   More widespread adoption of low-bit quantization for LLMs.
*   Further research into SVD-based techniques for LLM compression.
*   Development of new parameter-efficient fine-tuning methods.

**Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, I assign the paper a score of **7.5**.

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a relatively novel and practical approach to a significant problem in LLM quantization. The combination of SVD-based activation decomposition and parameter-efficient fine-tuning is promising. However, the limited ablation studies and efficiency analysis prevent a higher score.  The performance improvements, while good, aren't revolutionary compared to other recent techniques. The method's dependence on calibration data also introduces a potential limitation that requires further investigation. Still, it's a valuable contribution that warrants further exploration and development.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 0/10

### Data-driven Mesoscale Weather Forecasting Combining Swin-Unet and Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.19354v1)
- **Authors**: Yuta Hirabayashi, Daisuke Matsuoka
- **Abstract**: Data-driven weather prediction models exhibit promising performance and advance continuously. In particular, diffusion models represent fine-scale details without spatial smoothing, which is crucial for mesoscale predictions, such as heavy rainfall forecasting. However, the applications of diffusion models to mesoscale prediction remain limited. To address this gap, this study proposes an architecture that combines a diffusion model with Swin-Unet as a deterministic model, achieving mesoscale predictions while maintaining flexibility. The proposed architecture trains the two models independently, allowing the diffusion model to remain unchanged when the deterministic model is updated. Comparisons using the Fractions Skill Score and power spectral analysis demonstrate that incorporating the diffusion model leads to improved accuracy compared to predictions without it. These findings underscore the potential of the proposed architecture to enhance mesoscale predictions, particularly for strong rainfall events, while maintaining flexibility.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a data-driven mesoscale weather forecasting model that combines a Swin-Unet architecture as a deterministic predictor and a diffusion model as a probabilistic corrector. The key idea is to train these two models independently, allowing for flexible updates to the predictor without retraining the corrector. The approach aims to enhance high spatial frequency components in weather predictions, particularly for strong rainfall events. The authors demonstrate, using Fraction Skill Score (FSS) and power spectral analysis, that the proposed architecture improves accuracy compared to using the Swin-Unet predictor alone. The paper focuses on a case study of a heavy rainfall event influenced by a typhoon and a low-pressure system.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty:** The integration of a diffusion model as a *corrector* to enhance a deterministic forecasting model (Swin-Unet in this case) is a relatively novel approach. The independent training of the predictor and corrector provides significant flexibility, which is a major advantage over existing similar approaches (e.g., StormCast).
    *   **Significance:** Mesoscale weather forecasting, particularly for heavy rainfall, is a critical problem. Improvements in accuracy have direct societal benefits. The focus on enhancing high spatial frequency components is relevant, as these details are often crucial for predicting localized weather events.
    *   **Methodology:** The approach appears sound.  Using a transformer-based (Swin-Unet) architecture for the predictor is well-justified. The use of diffusion models to address limitations in capturing fine-scale details is a logical application of this technology. The metrics used (FSS, RMSE, power spectral analysis) are appropriate for evaluating the performance of weather forecasting models.
    *   **Results:** The case study provides convincing evidence that the proposed approach improves precipitation forecasts, especially for strong rainfall events. The power spectral analysis corroborates that the diffusion model enhances high spatial frequency components. The results show the diffusion model helps to correct the spatial smoothing problem of the Swin-Unet when used independently.
    *   **Flexibility:** The independent training of the Predictor and Corrector is a key strength and could significantly accelerate the adoption of new baseline weather models, as they can be added without retraining the Corrector.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** The evaluation is primarily based on a single case study. While the results are promising, a more comprehensive evaluation across a wider range of weather events and geographical locations is needed to demonstrate the generalizability of the approach.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The noise level parameter *σ* and the point at which the power spectra drops by a certain portion may be very sensitive to the quality of data.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The paper doesn't provide a clear analysis of the computational cost associated with training and running the diffusion-based corrector. Diffusion models can be computationally expensive, so an assessment of the trade-off between accuracy and computational resources is crucial.
    *   **Limited RMSE Improvement:** The RMSE improvements are limited for some variables, which may indicate that the corrector has variable-dependent performance.

*   **Novelty and Significance in context:**
The independent training of the predictor and corrector is a unique contribution of this study. Other existing works, such as ArchesWeatherGen and StormCast, require the training of their Corrector alongside with the output of Predictor, thereby limiting flexibility. In this way, this study can be thought of as an extension of StormCast that focuses on enhancing flexibility.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a promising approach with clear potential for improving mesoscale weather forecasting. The independent training aspect is a valuable contribution, offering a practical advantage in terms of model development and maintenance. The results, while limited to a single case study, are encouraging and support the core claims. However, the lack of a comprehensive evaluation and computational cost analysis, combined with the limited improvement in RMSE for certain variables, prevents a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Correcting Deviations from Normality: A Reformulated Diffusion Model for Multi-Class Unsupervised Anomaly Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.19357v1)
- **Authors**: Farzad Beizaee, Gregory A. Lodygensky, Christian Desrosiers, Jose Dolz
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in diffusion models have spurred research into their application for Reconstruction-based unsupervised anomaly detection. However, these methods may struggle with maintaining structural integrity and recovering the anomaly-free content of abnormal regions, especially in multi-class scenarios. Furthermore, diffusion models are inherently designed to generate images from pure noise and struggle to selectively alter anomalous regions of an image while preserving normal ones. This leads to potential degradation of normal regions during reconstruction, hampering the effectiveness of anomaly detection. This paper introduces a reformulation of the standard diffusion model geared toward selective region alteration, allowing the accurate identification of anomalies. By modeling anomalies as noise in the latent space, our proposed Deviation correction diffusion (DeCo-Diff) model preserves the normal regions and encourages transformations exclusively on anomalous areas. This selective approach enhances the reconstruction quality, facilitating effective unsupervised detection and localization of anomaly regions. Comprehensive evaluations demonstrate the superiority of our method in accurately identifying and localizing anomalies in complex images, with pixel-level AUPRC improvements of 11-14% over state-of-the-art models on well known anomaly detection datasets. The code is available at https://github.com/farzad-bz/DeCo-Diff
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "DeCo-Diff," a reformulated diffusion model designed for multi-class unsupervised anomaly detection (UAD). Unlike standard diffusion models that generate images from noise, DeCo-Diff focuses on selectively correcting deviations from normality in an input image. It models anomalies as "noise" in the latent space and aims to preserve normal regions while transforming only anomalous areas during reconstruction. The method uses a masking strategy during the forward diffusion process and a novel training objective to predict the direction of deviation (DoD) from normality. The authors claim their approach enhances reconstruction quality and facilitates more accurate anomaly detection and localization, demonstrating superior performance on MVTec-AD and VisA datasets compared to state-of-the-art methods. The method reformulates DDIM sampling for faster and more deterministic inference.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of reformulating diffusion models for *selective* anomaly correction is the paper's primary contribution and is relatively novel within the UAD space. Traditional diffusion-based UAD methods tend to operate globally, potentially degrading normal regions, which DeCo-Diff aims to address. The masking strategy to preserve details in normal areas is a good idea to improve the reconstruction of abnormal regions. Reformulating DDIM for faster, stable, deterministic sampling is also a solid contribution.

*   **Significance:** The problem of multi-class UAD is important, and improving its accuracy and efficiency is valuable. The reported gains in pixel-level AUPRC (11-14% over state-of-the-art) on standard benchmarks suggest that DeCo-Diff may represent a meaningful advance. If these results translate to real-world applications, the significance could be considerable. The faster and more deterministic sampling from the DDIM adaptation also has practical implications for deployment.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly identifies a limitation of existing diffusion-based UAD approaches: their indiscriminate alteration of normal and anomalous regions.
    *   The proposed solution, DeCo-Diff, is well-motivated and technically sound. The idea of modeling anomalies as noise in the latent space is clever.
    *   The masking strategy seems effective in preserving normal image details.
    *   The extensive experimental results on MVTec-AD and VisA are a strong point, particularly the substantial improvement in pixel-level AUPRC. The additional datasets strengthen the paper.
    *   The visualization of the deviation correction process (Figure 4) provides valuable insight into how DeCo-Diff works.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Some of the results show the second best performances. The paper does not explore *why* there are instances where other methods are better than DeCo-Diff. What are the specific characteristics of these instances? (Though it is the best approach *overall*).
    *   The reported results seem promising, but it's not very obvious *how* the proposed method differs from the approach proposed in [21].
    *   While the authors claim faster sampling, the paper lacks quantitative results about the speedup. Reporting inference time would make the results more complete.
    *   The failure cases are discussed, but more analysis is needed. For example, it's stated that small anomalies are sometimes missed because the VAE's variance causes them to be lost. However, no suggestions are mentioned to improve it, which may undermine the paper's contributions.
    *   The results on real world datasets need more explanation. In the current version, results in Table 7 do not have a great improvement.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in unsupervised anomaly detection, particularly in the development of more selective and efficient diffusion-based methods. The concepts introduced (deviation correction, masked forward diffusion) may inspire new UAD architectures.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the points above, the paper introduces a reasonably novel approach to UAD with promising empirical results on standard benchmarks and additional real-world datasets. The pixel-level AUPRC improvements are particularly notable. However, the weaknesses regarding failure cases, the lack of comparison with [21] approach, and the lack of experimental results about speedup need to be addressed. Moreover, improvements on real-world datasets need to be further investigated. Therefore, while the paper demonstrates a solid contribution, more comprehensive evaluation of failure modes and comparison with alternatives, would be appreciated.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### EfficientMT: Efficient Temporal Adaptation for Motion Transfer in Text-to-Video Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.19369v1)
- **Authors**: Yufei Cai, Hu Han, Yuxiang Wei, Shiguang Shan, Xilin Chen
- **Abstract**: The progress on generative models has led to significant advances on text-to-video (T2V) generation, yet the motion controllability of generated videos remains limited. Existing motion transfer methods explored the motion representations of reference videos to guide generation. Nevertheless, these methods typically rely on sample-specific optimization strategy, resulting in high computational burdens. In this paper, we propose \textbf{EfficientMT}, a novel and efficient end-to-end framework for video motion transfer. By leveraging a small set of synthetic paired motion transfer samples, EfficientMT effectively adapts a pretrained T2V model into a general motion transfer framework that can accurately capture and reproduce diverse motion patterns. Specifically, we repurpose the backbone of the T2V model to extract temporal information from reference videos, and further propose a scaler module to distill motion-related information. Subsequently, we introduce a temporal integration mechanism that seamlessly incorporates reference motion features into the video generation process. After training on our self-collected synthetic paired samples, EfficientMT enables general video motion transfer without requiring test-time optimization. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our EfficientMT outperforms existing methods in efficiency while maintaining flexible motion controllability. Our code will be available https://github.com/PrototypeNx/EfficientMT.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EfficientMT, a novel and efficient end-to-end framework for video motion transfer in text-to-video (T2V) diffusion models.  The method adapts a pre-trained T2V model to achieve motion transfer using a small set of synthetic paired motion transfer samples. It reuses the backbone of the T2V model to extract temporal information from reference videos and introduces a scaler module to distill motion-related information. A temporal integration mechanism incorporates the reference motion features into the video generation process.  The method is trained on synthetic paired samples and achieves general video motion transfer without test-time optimization, offering faster generation and flexible motion controllability compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**
    * **Efficiency:** The core strength is the emphasis on efficient adaptation, avoiding computationally expensive sample-specific optimization.  The end-to-end training using a relatively small synthetic dataset is a significant advantage.
    * **Motion Controllability:**  The method demonstrates flexible motion control and transfer, reproducing diverse motion patterns from reference videos.
    * **Design Choices:**  Reusing the pre-trained T2V backbone for feature extraction is a smart way to leverage existing knowledge and minimize training. The scaler module for distilling motion-related information is also a novel contribution.
    * **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper presents extensive experiments comparing EfficientMT with a variety of existing methods, using both qualitative and quantitative metrics. User studies also support the claims.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on Synthetic Data:** The method still relies on synthetic data for training the motion transfer. While the synthetic dataset is small, the quality and realism of this synthetic data significantly impact performance. The data construction process needs more detailed scrutiny. The effectiveness of cleaning and filtering synthetic data shows the dependence on the quality of synthetic data.
    * **Limited Discussion on Failure Cases:**  The paper acknowledges limitations with violently changing motion patterns leading to tearing artifacts, but a more in-depth discussion of other failure cases would strengthen the analysis.
    * **Limited Novelty:** While the components (scaler, integration mechanism) are well-engineered, they leverage existing concepts in diffusion models. The novelty comes more from the *combination* of these components in an efficient adaptation framework rather than breakthrough algorithmic advances.
    * **Incremental Advance:**  The performance gains compared to some existing methods (particularly MotionClone) might be incremental rather than revolutionary. The trade-off between efficiency and motion fidelity may be an important consideration.

* **Novelty and Significance:**
    * The idea of efficiently adapting a pre-trained T2V model for motion transfer using a relatively small amount of synthetic data is a valuable contribution.
    * The proposed scaler module and temporal integration mechanism demonstrate sound engineering and achieve good results.
    * The work addresses a critical problem in video generation: the ability to control motion efficiently and accurately.

* **Potential Influence:**
    * EfficientMT could influence future research on video motion transfer by highlighting the importance of efficient adaptation strategies.
    * The framework could be extended and improved upon by incorporating more sophisticated techniques for synthetic data generation or by exploring different motion representations.
    * The focus on efficiency makes the method practical for real-world applications.

* **Justification for Score:**

While EfficientMT offers a compelling approach to video motion transfer with a focus on efficiency, the paper's novelty is primarily in the combination of existing techniques. The reliance on synthetic data is a potential bottleneck, and the performance gains compared to existing methods might be incremental in certain cases. These limitations prevent it from achieving a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### DeClotH: Decomposable 3D Cloth and Human Body Reconstruction from a Single Image
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.19373v1)
- **Authors**: Hyeongjin Nam, Donghwan Kim, Jeongtaek Oh, Kyoung Mu Lee
- **Abstract**: Most existing methods of 3D clothed human reconstruction from a single image treat the clothed human as a single object without distinguishing between cloth and human body. In this regard, we present DeClotH, which separately reconstructs 3D cloth and human body from a single image. This task remains largely unexplored due to the extreme occlusion between cloth and the human body, making it challenging to infer accurate geometries and textures. Moreover, while recent 3D human reconstruction methods have achieved impressive results using text-to-image diffusion models, directly applying such an approach to this problem often leads to incorrect guidance, particularly in reconstructing 3D cloth. To address these challenges, we propose two core designs in our framework. First, to alleviate the occlusion issue, we leverage 3D template models of cloth and human body as regularizations, which provide strong geometric priors to prevent erroneous reconstruction by the occlusion. Second, we introduce a cloth diffusion model specifically designed to provide contextual information about cloth appearance, thereby enhancing the reconstruction of 3D cloth. Qualitative and quantitative experiments demonstrate that our proposed approach is highly effective in reconstructing both 3D cloth and the human body. More qualitative results are provided at https://hygenie1228.github.io/DeClotH/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "DeClotH: Decomposable 3D Cloth and Human Body Reconstruction from a Single Image":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DeClotH, a novel framework for reconstructing separate 3D models of cloth and human body from a single image.  Unlike existing methods that treat the clothed human as a monolithic entity, DeClotH addresses the problem of severe occlusion between the cloth and the human body. It does so by using 3D template models as strong geometric priors and introducing a custom cloth diffusion model (ClothDiffusion). ClothDiffusion is trained to generate cloth-specific images and can be controlled by 3D template information, leading to more accurate cloth reconstruction. The paper demonstrates qualitative and quantitative improvements over existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The core novelty lies in tackling the *decomposable* clothed human reconstruction problem head-on. While previous works have reconstructed clothed humans as a single mesh, DeClotH specifically aims to disentangle the cloth and body geometries, offering more flexibility for downstream tasks like virtual try-on. The design choices of using 3D templates as priors for regularization and introducing a cloth-specific diffusion model are also novel and contribute to the improvement in reconstruction quality. The ClothDiffusion model is a crucial addition. Existing diffusion models aren't optimized for cloth-specific generation, resulting in incorrect guidance.

**Significance:**

The ability to decompose a clothed human into separate cloth and body models has significant implications.  It opens up avenues for:

*   **Virtual Try-On:**  Easily swapping and manipulating clothing items on a virtual body.
*   **AR/VR Applications:** More realistic and controllable avatars for augmented and virtual reality experiences.
*   **Dataset Generation:** Creating synthetic training data with controllable cloth and body variations.
*   **Fashion design:** Allowing designers to experiment with clothing on different body types with ease.

**Strengths:**

*   **Problem Formulation:** Clearly identifies and addresses a limitation of existing clothed human reconstruction methods.
*   **Technical Design:**  The combination of template regularization and a cloth-specific diffusion model is well-reasoned and effective. The choice to train a specialized cloth diffusion model instead of directly using existing text-to-image models demonstrates a deep understanding of the problem.
*   **Experimental Evaluation:** The paper demonstrates quantitative improvements in reconstruction quality and provides qualitative results highlighting the benefits of the proposed approach. The ablation studies are well-designed and clearly show the impact of each component.
*   **Strong quantitative improvements**: The result on a challenging task is a great addition.
*   **Decomposition ability**: The ability of the work is not only to reconstruct clothing but to reconstruct it in a decomposable manner.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependence on Templates:**  The reliance on 3D template models, while providing regularization, also inherently limits the diversity and fidelity of the reconstructed cloth. The paper acknowledges this limitation, but it remains a concern. In other words, a limitation to represent cloth types (e.g., dresses) using existing template models.
*   **Inter-penetration issue:** There is inter-penetration between the cloth and the human body.
*   **Limited Evaluation on Diverse Clothing:** The evaluation could be strengthened by including a more diverse set of clothing types beyond those readily represented by current template models.
*   **Computational Cost:**  The use of diffusion models and optimization procedures can be computationally expensive, and the paper lacks detailed information about the run time.

**Potential Influence:**

DeClotH is likely to spur further research in decomposable clothed human reconstruction. The ideas of template regularization and cloth-specific diffusion models can be adopted and extended by other researchers. The work could also influence the development of more expressive and controllable clothed human avatars.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a significant contribution to the field. While the dependence on template models and computational cost represent areas for improvement, the problem formulation, technical design, experimental validation, and potential impact of DeClotH warrant a high score. It offers a novel framework that pushes the boundaries of clothed human reconstruction and has the potential to enable new applications in computer graphics and computer vision.

- **Score**: 8/10

### Interpretable Generative Models through Post-hoc Concept Bottlenecks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.19377v1)
- **Authors**: Akshay Kulkarni, Ge Yan, Chung-En Sun, Tuomas Oikarinen, Tsui-Wei Weng
- **Abstract**: Concept bottleneck models (CBM) aim to produce inherently interpretable models that rely on human-understandable concepts for their predictions. However, existing approaches to design interpretable generative models based on CBMs are not yet efficient and scalable, as they require expensive generative model training from scratch as well as real images with labor-intensive concept supervision. To address these challenges, we present two novel and low-cost methods to build interpretable generative models through post-hoc techniques and we name our approaches: concept-bottleneck autoencoder (CB-AE) and concept controller (CC). Our proposed approaches enable efficient and scalable training without the need of real data and require only minimal to no concept supervision. Additionally, our methods generalize across modern generative model families including generative adversarial networks and diffusion models. We demonstrate the superior interpretability and steerability of our methods on numerous standard datasets like CelebA, CelebA-HQ, and CUB with large improvements (average ~25%) over the prior work, while being 4-15x faster to train. Finally, a large-scale user study is performed to validate the interpretability and steerability of our methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of building interpretable generative models by introducing two novel post-hoc techniques: concept-bottleneck autoencoder (CB-AE) and concept controller (CC).  Instead of training a generative model from scratch with concept supervision (which is computationally expensive and requires labeled real images), the proposed methods leverage pre-trained generative models and introduce a concept bottleneck to enable interpretability and steerability. CB-AE reconstructs the generator's latent space through a concept space, while CC provides concept predictions and leverages optimization-based interventions. Experiments on CelebA, CelebA-HQ, and CUB datasets demonstrate superior interpretability and steerability compared to existing approaches, with significant speed improvements. A large-scale user study validates the effectiveness of the methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The primary novelty lies in the *post-hoc* nature of the approach.  Existing concept bottleneck generative models require training from scratch, making them less scalable. By inserting a concept bottleneck into a *pre-trained* generator, the authors significantly reduce the computational burden and reliance on labeled real data.  The introduction of both CB-AE and the simplified CC, along with the optimization-based intervention strategy, adds further novel elements.  While concept bottleneck models and generative model interpretability have been explored previously, the specific combination of post-hoc application and optimization-based intervention is innovative.

* **Significance:**  The paper's significance stems from its practical benefits.  The faster training times (4-15x faster) and reduced reliance on labeled data make interpretable generative models more accessible and scalable.  The improved steerability offers a tangible advantage in controlling the generation process. The application of these methods to diverse generative model families (GANs and diffusion models) and datasets demonstrates their versatility.  A large-scale user study adds weight to the claim that the methods improve interpretability from a human perspective. The ability to apply these techniques to pre-trained models is a major benefit for real-world applications where retraining from scratch is often infeasible. The paper demonstrates improvements over a previous approach, CBGM, in steerability while drastically cutting down the training time.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Efficiency:** The methods are significantly faster to train than prior work.
    * **Scalability:** Avoids the need for retraining the entire generative model and reduces reliance on expensive real data.
    * **Versatility:** Works across different generative model architectures and datasets.
    * **Interpretability:**  Improves concept accuracy and steerability.
    * **User Study:** Includes a user study to validate the methods' effectiveness.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Image Quality Trade-off:** The CB-AE exhibits a relatively higher drop in image quality compared to the base model without the bottleneck.  This is a crucial consideration for generative models where fidelity is paramount. Although optimization based interventions can recover image quality it adds computational cost.
    * **Dependency on Pseudo-Labels:**  The methods rely on pseudo-labels generated by CLIP or supervised classifiers. The quality of these pseudo-labels directly affects the performance of the CB-AE and CC.  While the authors demonstrate the effectiveness of their approach, the choice of pseudo-labeling technique is a critical factor. Although they show improvement using TIP-fs this comes at an increase in human supervision.
    * **Steerability Limitations:** While steerability is improved, the limitations section acknowledges that changes to concepts *outside* the known set are not adequately addressed by the steerability metric. It would be even stronger if the authors were to explore limitations in concept orthogonalization in these models and what that means for interventions.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in interpretable machine learning, generative models, and controllable AI. It offers a practical and efficient way to build interpretable generative models, making them more accessible for various applications. The post-hoc nature of the approach is particularly appealing, as it allows researchers to leverage existing pre-trained models. Further research could explore ways to improve image quality and address the limitations regarding out-of-set concepts.
* **Rigour:** Claims are well supported with thorough experiments, ablation studies, and comparative evaluations against relevant baselines. The inclusion of a user study strengthens the paper's arguments. The authors also discuss limitations candidly.

**Score:** 8

**Rationale:** The paper presents a significant advancement in the field of interpretable generative models by introducing a novel and practical post-hoc approach. The methods are efficient, scalable, and versatile, making them appealing for real-world applications. The demonstrated improvements in steerability and the validation through a user study contribute to the paper's impact. The primary limitation lies in the tradeoff with image quality and the dependence on pseudo-labels. However, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses, justifying a high score. Given the practical nature and broad applicability of this technique, it is likely to influence other researchers and lead to further innovation within the field. Although there are some limitations the authors do well to openly admit to them, and the paper achieves solid improvements using an innovative approach.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### MVPortrait: Text-Guided Motion and Emotion Control for Multi-view Vivid Portrait Animation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.19383v1)
- **Authors**: Yukang Lin, Hokit Fung, Jianjin Xu, Zeping Ren, Adela S. M. Lau, Guosheng Yin, Xiu Li
- **Abstract**: Recent portrait animation methods have made significant strides in generating realistic lip synchronization. However, they often lack explicit control over head movements and facial expressions, and cannot produce videos from multiple viewpoints, resulting in less controllable and expressive animations. Moreover, text-guided portrait animation remains underexplored, despite its user-friendly nature. We present a novel two-stage text-guided framework, MVPortrait (Multi-view Vivid Portrait), to generate expressive multi-view portrait animations that faithfully capture the described motion and emotion. MVPortrait is the first to introduce FLAME as an intermediate representation, effectively embedding facial movements, expressions, and view transformations within its parameter space. In the first stage, we separately train the FLAME motion and emotion diffusion models based on text input. In the second stage, we train a multi-view video generation model conditioned on a reference portrait image and multi-view FLAME rendering sequences from the first stage. Experimental results exhibit that MVPortrait outperforms existing methods in terms of motion and emotion control, as well as view consistency. Furthermore, by leveraging FLAME as a bridge, MVPortrait becomes the first controllable portrait animation framework that is compatible with text, speech, and video as driving signals.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MVPortrait, a novel two-stage framework for generating expressive multi-view portrait animations guided by text. The key idea is to use FLAME (a 3D parametric face model) as an intermediate representation to control facial motion, expression, and view transformations.  The first stage, Text2FLAME, consists of two diffusion models (MotionDM and EmotionDM) trained separately to generate FLAME pose and expression parameters from text descriptions. The second stage, FLAME2Video, generates multi-view video frames conditioned on a reference portrait image and the FLAME sequences rendered from multiple viewpoints. The framework is designed to be compatible with text, speech, and video as driving signals, and incorporates a view attention mechanism to ensure consistency across multiple viewpoints. Experiments demonstrate superior control over motion and emotion, as well as improved view consistency, compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel components. The most significant is the integration of FLAME as an intermediate representation within a text-guided portrait animation framework. This is innovative because it allows explicit control over pose and expression parameters based on text input. The separation of motion and emotion diffusion models (MotionDM and EmotionDM) also contributes to the framework's controllability. Extending the animation to multi-view using a FLAME-based rendering approach and incorporating a view attention mechanism further enhance the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses limitations of previous portrait animation methods, such as a lack of explicit control over head movements and facial expressions, the inability to generate multi-view videos, and the relative lack of exploration into text-guided animation. By offering greater control and multi-view generation, MVPortrait has the potential to be beneficial for avatar creation, virtual communication, and entertainment. The framework's compatibility with various driving signals (text, audio, video) significantly increases its versatility and user-friendliness.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the shortcomings of existing methods.
    *   **Well-Defined Approach:** The two-stage framework and the use of FLAME are logically presented.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper includes quantitative and qualitative evaluations, comparing against relevant baselines and demonstrating the effectiveness of the individual components through ablation studies. The inclusion of a user study strengthens the evaluation.
    *   **Versatility:** The framework's adaptability to different driving signals, as shown in the diagram and briefly discussed, indicates a strong design.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on FLAME:** The framework's performance is inherently limited by the accuracy and expressiveness of the FLAME model.
    *   **Limited Dataset:** The reliance on a relatively small subset of the RenderMe-360 dataset limits its generalizability and performance with larger and more diverse datasets. The paper could benefit from demonstrating results on more challenging and diverse datasets.
    *   **Computational Cost:** While the paper provides timings, the required computational resources for training (8 A100 GPUs for several days) make this framework less accessible and reproducible for many researchers.
    *   **Lack of comparison to text-based 3D generation.** With the advent of 3D face reconstruction, it would be beneficial to compare and discuss the advantage of this method over directly generating textures and models using text.
    *   **Ablation could be more comprehensive.** While it includes text to FLAME and FLAME2Video, a component breakdown for each of those sections may improve comprehension.

*   **Potential Impact:**  MVPortrait has the potential to influence the field of portrait animation by introducing a more controllable and versatile framework. By using FLAME effectively, the model is able to integrate multiple aspects such as facial motion, identity, expression and camera viewpoint. The framework's modular design could serve as a basis for future research in this area.

**Justification for the Score:**

MVPortrait presents a well-designed, innovative framework with solid results. The use of FLAME as an intermediate representation and the separated motion/emotion control contribute significantly to its capabilities. While some limitations exist in terms of dataset size, computational cost, and dependence on FLAME's capabilities, the approach's versatility and controllability outweigh these concerns. It is definitely a solid addition to the field.

### Inference-Time Scaling for Flow Models via Stochastic Generation and Rollover Budget Forcing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.19385v1)
- **Authors**: Jaihoon Kim, Taehoon Yoon, Jisung Hwang, Minhyuk Sung
- **Abstract**: We propose an inference-time scaling approach for pretrained flow models. Recently, inference-time scaling has gained significant attention in LLMs and diffusion models, improving sample quality or better aligning outputs with user preferences by leveraging additional computation. For diffusion models, particle sampling has allowed more efficient scaling due to the stochasticity at intermediate denoising steps. On the contrary, while flow models have gained popularity as an alternative to diffusion models--offering faster generation and high-quality outputs in state-of-the-art image and video generative models--efficient inference-time scaling methods used for diffusion models cannot be directly applied due to their deterministic generative process. To enable efficient inference-time scaling for flow models, we propose three key ideas: 1) SDE-based generation, enabling particle sampling in flow models, 2) Interpolant conversion, broadening the search space and enhancing sample diversity, and 3) Rollover Budget Forcing (RBF), an adaptive allocation of computational resources across timesteps to maximize budget utilization. Our experiments show that SDE-based generation, particularly variance-preserving (VP) interpolant-based generation, improves the performance of particle sampling methods for inference-time scaling in flow models. Additionally, we demonstrate that RBF with VP-SDE achieves the best performance, outperforming all previous inference-time scaling approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces an inference-time scaling method for pre-trained flow models, aiming to improve sample quality and alignment with user preferences. The core idea revolves around enabling particle sampling, traditionally used with diffusion models, in the context of flow models, which are deterministic. This is achieved through three key innovations: (1) converting the deterministic generative process of flow models into a stochastic one via SDE-based generation; (2) employing a Variance-Preserving (VP) interpolant to broaden the search space, replacing the typical linear interpolant used in flow models; and (3) implementing a Rollover Budget Forcing (RBF) mechanism for adaptive allocation of computational resources (NFEs) across timesteps. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of their method on challenging tasks like compositional text-to-image generation and quantity-aware image generation, showing it outperforms previous inference-time scaling approaches, including diffusion models with comparable or even more computational resources.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its adaptation of techniques from diffusion models (namely, particle sampling and VP interpolants) to the flow model paradigm. While individual components might not be entirely novel in isolation, their combination within the flow model context, particularly addressing the deterministic nature of flow models, is innovative. The RBF mechanism is also a noteworthy contribution, offering a practical solution for efficient compute utilization.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from addressing a limitation of flow models regarding inference-time scaling. By enabling particle sampling, the authors provide a pathway to improved performance and user alignment, potentially broadening the applicability of flow models in generative tasks. The superior performance compared to diffusion models with similar or higher computational budgets highlights the potential for efficient and high-quality image synthesis using flow models. The improvement in compositional and quantity-aware generation tasks is significant because it shows the algorithm can meet specific details within the text prompt (something current models often struggle with).

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-explained technical details of the proposed method (SDE conversion, VP interpolant, RBF).
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation on challenging tasks.
    *   Comparison against strong baselines, including diffusion models and SoTA flow models.
    *   Ablation studies demonstrating the impact of each component (SDE, VP, RBF).
    *   Qualitative results supporting the quantitative findings.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The method relies on a pre-trained flow model. While using a pre-trained model is common, the results may be significantly different using a model with different architectures or training regimes.
    *   The VP interpolant conversion leverages velocity information from the original model's linear interpolation to guide its search in a different space. While this seems advantageous, it may potentially restrict the search to a space heavily influenced by the original model, not truly deviating from its "knowledge."  This "influence bias" should be carefully studied.
    *   The experimental results, while positive, could benefit from broader benchmarking.  Evaluation on additional datasets or generation tasks would strengthen the claims.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:

    *   Encouraging further research on inference-time scaling techniques for flow models.
    *   Inspiring the transfer of techniques from diffusion models to flow models and vice versa.
    *   Providing a practical solution for efficient and controllable image synthesis using flow models.
    *   Serving as a basis for developing more advanced control mechanisms for generative models.

* **Detailed Reasoning for the assigned score:** The modifications made to the flow models are novel and practical, enabling flow models to be leveraged for compositional generation with better performance than diffusion models in some cases. While not groundbreaking, this modification enables the flow model to be used in a new domain, which is high-impact.

- **Score**: 7/10

### DeCAP: Context-Adaptive Prompt Generation for Debiasing Zero-shot Question Answering in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.19426v1)
- **Authors**: Suyoung Bae, YunSeok Choi, Jee-Hyong Lee
- **Abstract**: While Large Language Models (LLMs) excel in zero-shot Question Answering (QA), they tend to expose biases in their internal knowledge when faced with socially sensitive questions, leading to a degradation in performance. Existing zero-shot methods are efficient but fail to consider context and prevent bias propagation in the answers. To address this, we propose DeCAP, a method for debiasing LLMs using Context-Adaptive Prompt Generation. DeCAP leverages a Question Ambiguity Detection to take appropriate debiasing actions based on the context and a Neutral Answer Guidance Generation to suppress the LLMs make objective judgments about the context, minimizing the propagation of bias from their internal knowledge. Our various experiments across eight LLMs show that DeCAP achieves state-of-the-art zero-shot debiased QA performance. This demonstrates DeCAP's efficacy in enhancing the fairness and accuracy of LLMs in diverse QA settings.
- **Summary**: Okay, I can provide a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper "DeCAP: Context-Adaptive Prompt Generation for Debiasing Zero-shot Question Answering in Large Language Models."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DeCAP, a novel zero-shot debiasing method for Question Answering (QA) in Large Language Models (LLMs). DeCAP addresses the limitations of existing approaches that either use fixed debiasing instructions or rely on the LLM's potentially biased self-explanations. DeCAP consists of two main components: (1) a Question Ambiguity Detection module that classifies questions based on their context, adapting the instruction accordingly (to either encourage answering or encourage not answering relying on potentially biased prior knowledge), and (2) a Neutral Answer Guidance Generation module that leverages external knowledge to generate neutral context to steer the LLM towards objective judgments and minimize bias propagation. The authors demonstrate DeCAP's effectiveness across various LLMs and two QA benchmarks, showing improved accuracy and reduced bias compared to existing zero-shot debiasing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using a context-adaptive approach is a valuable step forward. While fixed instructions can sometimes backfire, adapting the response based on question ambiguity makes intuitive sense. The two-pronged approach of ambiguity detection and neutral answer guidance has novelty, especially their design of relying on reasoning-context similarity and leveraging external knowledge for objective judgements, respectively.
*   **Significance:** The problem of bias in LLMs is a significant one. Reducing biases in zero-shot QA tasks is particularly valuable because it doesn't require retraining the model, making it more accessible and practical. The approach seems promising and the results back it up. Their approach is relatively simple to implement and could be integrated into various QA systems without significant overhead, increasing adoption.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   The method achieves state-of-the-art zero-shot debiased QA performance.
    *   The context-adaptive prompt generation strategy is well-motivated and addresses limitations of previous methods.
    *   The experimental results are comprehensive, with evaluations across multiple LLMs and two QA benchmarks.
    *   Ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component of DeCAP.
    *   The analysis of how performance varies based on categories of bias, and based on question difficulty, is relatively strong.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on ROUGE score for ambiguity detection, while efficient, might not be the most sophisticated approach. More advanced semantic similarity measures *might* improve performance, at the expense of efficiency.
    *   While ChatGPT is used for evaluation, *some* further human evaluation would have increased trust in the effectiveness of the neutral response generation.
    *   The improvement on unambiguous questions seems less dramatic than on ambiguous ones (as expected), and in fact, seems to make the question perform *worse* compared to base (See table 3, for example).
    *   The "Qualitative Example" provided in Table 16 does not provide specific context (and thus the answer is more vague); stronger qualitative examples would have been better.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work has the potential to influence the development of more robust and fair QA systems. The context-adaptive prompt generation strategy could be adopted and extended to other tasks and domains. This research could also spark further investigation into more accurate and efficient methods for question ambiguity detection and neutral answer guidance generation.

**Justification for Score:**

DeCAP presents a valuable and practical contribution to the field of debiasing LLMs. The context-adaptive approach and the use of external knowledge for neutral guidance are novel ideas that address limitations of existing methods. The strong experimental results, comprehensive analysis, and clear writing style make the paper a solid contribution, and makes their system implementable. While the reliance on ROUGE score for ambiguity detection is not the *most* sophisticated, it appears to be more effective (or at least more consistently effective) than methods reliant on LLM's internal reasoning.

### ASP-VMUNet: Atrous Shifted Parallel Vision Mamba U-Net for Skin Lesion Segmentation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.19427v1)
- **Authors**: Muyi Bao, Shuchang Lyu, Zhaoyang Xu, Qi Zhao, Changyu Zeng, Wenpei Bai, Guangliang Cheng
- **Abstract**: Skin lesion segmentation is a critical challenge in computer vision, and it is essential to separate pathological features from healthy skin for diagnostics accurately. Traditional Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) are limited by narrow receptive fields, and Transformers face significant computational burdens. This paper presents a novel skin lesion segmentation framework, the Atrous Shifted Parallel Vision Mamba UNet (ASP-VMUNet), which integrates the efficient and scalable Mamba architecture to overcome limitations in traditional CNNs and computationally demanding Transformers. The framework introduces an atrous scan technique that minimizes background interference and expands the receptive field, enhancing Mamba's scanning capabilities. Additionally, the inclusion of a Parallel Vision Mamba (PVM) layer and a shift round operation optimizes feature segmentation and fosters rich inter-segment information exchange. A supplementary CNN branch with a Selective-Kernel (SK) Block further refines the segmentation by blending local and global contextual information. Tested on four benchmark datasets (ISIC16/17/18 and PH2), ASP-VMUNet demonstrates superior performance in skin lesion segmentation, validated by comprehensive ablation studies. This approach not only advances medical image segmentation but also highlights the benefits of hybrid architectures in medical imaging technology. Our code is available at https://github.com/BaoBao0926/ASP-VMUNet/tree/main.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ASP-VMUNet: Atrous Shifted Parallel Vision Mamba U-Net for Skin Lesion Segmentation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ASP-VMUNet, a novel U-Net architecture for skin lesion segmentation that leverages the Mamba architecture. The key innovations include: (1) an "atrous scan" technique designed to reduce the impact of less informative background patches and expand the receptive field, (2) a "shift round" operation to improve information exchange between feature segments in the parallel Mamba layers, and (3) the use of a Selective Kernel (SK) block to effectively fuse global (Mamba-derived) and local (CNN-derived) features.  The authors demonstrate superior performance of ASP-VMUNet compared to existing methods on several benchmark skin lesion datasets (ISIC16/17/18 and PH2). Ablation studies validate the contribution of each component.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces several novel components: the atrous scan, the shift-round operation, and the specific combination of Mamba with a CNN branch enhanced by the SK block within a U-Net framework. The Atrous Scan technique is particularly interesting, representing a novel sampling method different from the traditional global sampling. However, the individual components themselves (Mamba, U-Net, SK block, Atrous Convolutions) are not entirely novel; the novelty lies in their specific combination and adaptation for skin lesion segmentation. The "shift round" operation, while inspired by Swin-Transformer, is a reasonable adaptation for parallel Mamba blocks. The combination of Mamba and CNN is present in other models, but the explicit SK fusion appears unique here.

*   **Significance:** Skin lesion segmentation is a critical task for early diagnosis, and the paper addresses limitations of existing CNN and Transformer-based approaches. The demonstrated improvement in performance across several datasets, including ISIC, suggests a potential advancement in the field. The ASP-VMUNet architecture also highlights the benefits of hybrid architectures in medical imaging, which could inspire further research in this direction. The paper also provides a good categorization of Scan Strategies, highlighting Atrous Scan's contributions in that context.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper demonstrates SOTA performance on multiple datasets. The extensive ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component.
    *   **Well-Defined Innovation:** The Atrous Scan is conceptually clear and motivated, especially concerning skin lesion imagery.
    *   **Hybrid Approach:**  Successfully integrates CNN-like and Mamba elements with a well-motivated fusion strategy.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the integration is novel, the individual components are well-established. The improvement, while statistically significant, might not be practically transformative in all scenarios.
    *   **Limited Analysis of Atrous Scan:** While the results demonstrate the effectiveness of Atrous Scan, deeper analysis of its behavior, such as how different atrous rates affect receptive field and performance on different lesion types, would be valuable.  Why Atrous Scan works best on some datasets with S=8, while S=2 produces the best general results.
    *  **PH2 Limitations:** The results on the PH2 dataset should be interpreted carefully due to its relatively small size.
    * **Specificity Degradation:** As described by the authors, the specificity degradation in certain models could be further investigated, providing more insights into edge preservation.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of future segmentation models in medical imaging, particularly in exploring the synergy between Mamba and CNN architectures. The Atrous Scan technique could also be adopted or adapted in other applications where background noise or varying lesion sizes are a challenge.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a well-executed application of Mamba to skin lesion segmentation, with several notable innovations that contribute to improved performance. While the individual components are not revolutionary, the novel combination of the atrous scan, shift round, and SK fusion within the U-Net framework demonstrates a clear understanding of the problem and delivers tangible improvements. The strong empirical results and ablation studies support the claims of the paper. The limitation of incremental novelty, however, prevents the assignment of a higher score.

### Quantifying the Ease of Reproducing Training Data in Unconditional Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.19429v1)
- **Authors**: Masaya Hasegawa, Koji Yasuda
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models, which have been advancing rapidly in recent years, may generate samples that closely resemble the training data. This phenomenon, known as memorization, may lead to copyright issues. In this study, we propose a method to quantify the ease of reproducing training data in unconditional diffusion models. The average of a sample population following the Langevin equation in the reverse diffusion process moves according to a first-order ordinary differential equation (ODE). This ODE establishes a 1-to-1 correspondence between images and their noisy counterparts in the latent space. Since the ODE is reversible and the initial noisy images are sampled randomly, the volume of an image's projected area represents the probability of generating those images. We examined the ODE, which projects images to latent space, and succeeded in quantifying the ease of reproducing training data by measuring the volume growth rate in this process. Given the relatively low computational complexity of this method, it allows us to enhance the quality of training data by detecting and modifying the easily memorized training samples.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the paper "Quantifying the Ease of Reproducing Training Data in Unconditional Diffusion Models". Here's a summary and a critical evaluation.

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the issue of memorization in diffusion models, where generated samples closely resemble training data, potentially leading to copyright concerns. It proposes a novel method to quantify the "ease of reproduction" of training data in *unconditional* diffusion models. The core idea revolves around analyzing the Ordinary Differential Equation (ODE) that governs the average trajectory of samples in the reverse diffusion process (mapping images to the latent space). The paper hypothesizes that the volume growth rate of a small region surrounding a training sample in latent space is indicative of the probability of generating that image. Specifically, the paper quantifies the ease of image reproduction by measuring this volume growth rate. It then presents empirical results on overfitted models and compares memorized vs. unmemorized CIFAR10 images, showing a correlation between higher volume growth rates and ease of reproduction. Finally, the paper explores the parameter settings and demonstrates the method's robustness and the sufficiency of single-axis stretch rate.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using the volume growth rate along the ODE trajectory in the latent space to quantify memorization is reasonably novel. The connection between the trajectory volume growth in the latent space and probability of reproducing samples from the training dataset is a very good idea, and a practical one. While some works explore memorization in diffusion models, this paper proposes a distinct, geometric perspective by focusing on how the space around training examples evolves under the reverse diffusion process. The finding that single axis rate is enough is an interesting optimization for the algorithm.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in providing a practical, computationally efficient method to identify easily reproducible training data. This can be highly valuable for curating training datasets, detecting potentially problematic samples that might raise copyright issues, and mitigating overfitting. A quantifiable metric to determine how likely an image can be generated from random noise is definitely useful for copyright compliance and the data curation process. Furthermore, it also sheds light on the latent space structure of diffusion models, an area that remains somewhat opaque.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies a practical problem in diffusion models.
    *   **Novel Approach:** The method of analyzing volume growth rate in the latent space is original.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides empirical evidence supporting the proposed method.
    *   **Computational Efficiency:** The discovery of using a single-axis over single time step makes the method computationally efficient and applicable in practice.
    *   **Generalizability:** The proposed methodology is, as the authors claim, generic enough to be applied to any diffusion model with a deterministic reverse diffusion process.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Unconditional Diffusion Models Only:** The method is explicitly limited to unconditional diffusion models, meaning it's not directly applicable to more advanced *conditional* models (e.g., text-to-image models). Extending this to conditional models is non-trivial.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Justification:** While the intuition behind the volume growth rate is convincing, a more rigorous theoretical derivation of why and how it correlates to the ease of reproduction would be beneficial.
    *   **Dataset Specificity:** The experiments are primarily conducted on CIFAR10. Evaluating the approach on more diverse and complex datasets (e.g., ImageNet, LAION) would strengthen the findings.
    *   **Lack of comparison with Memorization Metrics:** Other metrics for memorization (such as distance based metrics that look for near-duplicates in the training set or generate samples and look at the L2 distance to the closest image in the training dataset) could be useful.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research on data curation for diffusion models and to stimulate further investigations into the structure of latent spaces. The computational efficiency could make it a valuable tool for practitioners. It could influence the development of methods to modify easily memorized training samples, further improving data privacy and copyright protection.

**Overall Score:**

Taking all of these factors into account, I assign the paper a score of **7**.

**Rationale for the Score:**

The paper presents a novel, practical, and potentially significant method for quantifying memorization in *unconditional* diffusion models. The geometric approach and empirical validation are strong points. However, the limitation to unconditional models, the need for more theoretical justification, dataset specificity, and lack of comparisons hold it back from a higher score. While the method is robust and efficient, the limited scope somewhat reduces its immediate impact on the broader field of diffusion model research. The paper opens interesting avenues for further exploration of the latent space and data curation strategies. However, more validation is required.
